# B-PLAN-CURRENCY-CHECK — PRE-IMPLEMENTATION AUDIT AND IMPLEMENTATION PLAN

**Ref audited:** `d4a2679c4` (other refs named where used: `9d80cd991`, `f837e4b87`, `faec7c6b2`, `51726bea6`; app alert code at the held staging ref `bc199185e`) · **change-class:** `non_architecture` · **Owner:** CC-A · **Scope:** `Claude Comms and Packages/Scope Files/B_PLAN_CURRENCY_CHECK_SCOPE.md` (Step 1 APPROVED r7; Langston's binding rulings are its §10, §10b, §10c) · **Gate:** Langston, Step 2 — **owed** (not yet dispatched)

> **How this document was built.** Five Step-2 audit reads, one per area, each at `d4a2679c4`, read-only on staging and Helsinki. Their findings, censuses, evidence and numbers are carried **verbatim** in §5 under an area prefix — `CD-` close-diff (OBJ-1/OBJ-2), `CE-` census and mistake pass (OBJ-3/OBJ-4), `DL-` deletions (OBJ-5/OBJ-6), `PF-` plan fixes (OBJ-8/OBJ-9), `HY-` checker hygiene and governance (OBJ-10). Inside a finding's or a source plan item's own text, a bare `A7` refers to a finding **of the same area** (the plan item's source file names the area). Where two areas disagree, both are shown and the disagreement is named — nothing is averaged; **the four COUNT disagreements (OPEN issues, dated homes, "after X" references, row 16's grammar) are settled by written rules in §1.1**, each implemented and run with controls, and each reader's own figure sits in §1 as a lettered row. Every plan item in §6 names the findings it falls out of; items with none are flagged `UNAUDITED`.
> **Fresh-reader record:** no fresh-reader round has been run on this document yet. Per `workflow-02-audit-and-plan`, the loop (claim-only rounds, then one object round at the ref) is owed before this goes to Langston.

---

### 1.1 THE FOUR RULES THAT SETTLE WHERE THE READERS DISAGREED

Written after the five reads, by one reconciler. Each rule is chosen by what its census list or objective is FOR, implemented in a scratch script, and run on the blob at the named ref (`git show <ref>:<path>`, stderr not suppressed), never the working tree. Scripts, in the session scratchpad and not committed: `recon/open_rule.py` (R1), `recon/dated_rule.py` (R2), `recon/after_rule.py` (R3), `recon/fg_rule.mjs` and `recon/listd.mjs` (R4, importing `config.mjs` from `d4a2679c4`). Each reader's own figure, and the rule that produced it, is a lettered row in §1 (rows 3a-3b, 6a-6b, 13a-13b, 23a-23c).

**R1 — OPEN issue** (census lists (b) and (c); the OBJ-3 baseline)
- **Rule.** A HEAD is a line of `1-system-manual/RUNNING_ISSUES.md` matching `^(?:#{2,4} |- \*\*|\*\*)#(\d+)\b(.*)$`. Its STATUS WORD: take group 2, then repeat until nothing changes — delete the leading run matching `[^A-Za-z(]+`; if the text now starts with `(`, delete through the matching balanced `)`. The word is the first `[A-Za-z]+` run, upper-cased (empty if none). An issue number is OPEN iff at least one of its heads has the word `OPEN` and none has a word in {`CLOSED`, `RESOLVED`, `WITHDRAWN`, `DONE`, `FIXED`}.
- **Why this rule.** List (b) exists to surface *"OPEN ledger entries with no placement"* (OBJ-3), so an OPEN issue misread as not-OPEN is invisible to it — the one error the census cannot afford. `#1108`'s head is `(RENUMBERED from #668 on 2026-09-29) OPEN 2026-08-07`: the parenthetical is a renumbering annotation, and the entry's own status word is OPEN.
- **Result.** **469** at `9d80cd991` (766 heads, 663 numbers) · **472** at `d4a2679c4` (770 heads, 667 numbers). The scope's Step-1 469 reproduces.
- **Controls.** Positive: `#1108` is OPEN (the one issue that turns on the parenthetical strip); `#570` is OPEN. Negative: `#906` (heads OPEN, WITHDRAWN, CLOSED) and `#907` (OPEN, WITHDRAWN) are not OPEN; `#482` (a `✅ RESOLVED` head plus an OPEN head) is not OPEN; `#581`, whose `(orig) OPEN` head now reads OPEN, stays not-OPEN through its `RESOLVED` head. The strip changes the word on 15 heads at each ref and the OPEN set on exactly one issue, `#1108`. Without the strip the rule gives 468 / 471 — the same set as PF's `startswith` rule (no other difference at either ref).
- **Population.** Distinct issue numbers with at least one head: 663 at `9d80cd991`, 667 at `d4a2679c4`. Reach limits, unchanged: a `#532`-shaped head (`CORE DEFECT CLOSED … ISSUE REMAINS OPEN`) reads not-OPEN (Question 27); a reused number (`#559`) merges two issues (Question 28).

**R2 — dated HOME** (OBJ-8; census list (c))
- **Rule.** For each OPEN issue (R1), its SPAN is every line from each of its heads to the line before the next head, stopping early at a `# ` or `## ` heading. A MATCH is an occurrence of `\bdue\b\W{0,3}(20\d\d-\d\d-\d\d)` (case-insensitive) on a span line that also contains `\bHOME\b` (case-sensitive), unless the character immediately before `due` is `"` or `` ` `` (QUOTED-STRUCK: the line quotes a date to record that it was struck). An issue is on the list iff it has at least one match.
- **Why this rule.** OBJ-8 hands *"homes that still carry a date"* to their owners, and §9.4's objection is a calendar due date standing in for a place. `#744` (`this entry READ "DUE 2026-09-05"`) and `#736` (`This line read "DUE 2026-09-11…"`, since PLACED) no longer carry a date as their home — handing them over would ask the owner to strike what is already struck. `#682`'s `DUE: 2026-08-12` is a due date whatever the punctuation; `\s+` misses it.
- **Result.** **30 issues / 39 lines / 41 matches** at both `9d80cd991` and `d4a2679c4`: `#443 #444 #448 #449 #452 #453 #455 #480 #481 #483 #484 #485 #486 #487 #492 #493 #494 #682 #696 #705 #738 #742 #743 #900 #901 #902 #903 #908 #909 #910`. The `2026-09-05` subset: **11 issues / 12 lines** (`#705 #738 #742 #743 #900-#903 #908 #909 #910`).
- **Controls.** Positive: `#738`'s upper-case `DUE 2026-09-05` (L4465) and `#682`'s `DUE: 2026-08-12` (L3582) are found. `#682` is found only because its line happens to contain `HOME` ("THE §13 NAMED HOME"); its three same-form siblings are not (reach limit below; §1 row 40). Negative: the three quoted-struck matches are excluded — `#744` L4376, `#736` L4505, `#705` L3723 (`#705` stays on the list through L3714); `#907`'s line is excluded (WITHDRAWN head), as is `#906` (WITHDRAWN, CLOSED). Sensitivity: `HOME` case-insensitive, or a span to the next head only, changes nothing; dropping the strike test gives 32 issues / 42 lines.
- **Population.** The `\bHOME\b` lines inside the spans of the 472 OPEN issues at `d4a2679c4` (469 at `9d80cd991`). Reach limits: only an ISO date right after `due` counts — `due: this week` (`#678`), `DUE: with the next alert…` (`#679`) and `due AT BATCH CLOSE` (`#686`) are not calendar dates and are not caught; a date on a HOME line without `due` is not caught either (198 OPEN issues have an ISO date somewhere on a HOME line at `d4a2679c4`, mostly placement and record dates — which is why `due` is the discriminator). Whether `#696`'s data-read date belongs on the list stays Question 25. ⚠️ **Reach limit found in round 1:** a due date on an `OWNER` line with no `HOME` is invisible. That is exactly three OPEN issues, all CC-B's: `#680` L3611 (`OWNER: CC-B. DUE: 2026-08-09`), `#681` L3629 and `#684` L3679/L3699 (both `2026-08-12`), the same at `9d80cd991` and `158c66983`. Counting an `OWNER` line as well gives 33 issues / 44 lines / 46 matches. Whether R2 is widened is put to Langston; until he rules, the three are named to CC-B in P16 and not counted.

**R3 — an "after X" reference that names no earlier row** (census list (e); OBJ-9's recount)
- **Rule.** A §4 ROW is a table line between `## 4.` and `## 5.` with exactly 7 cells whose first cell matches `^\d+[a-z]?$` (cells: # | item | batch | owner | status | report | note). In each row's NOTE cell take (i) every capture of `order.py:246`'s own regex `\bafter ([#A-Za-z0-9:._-]*[#:A-Z0-9][#A-Za-z0-9:._-]*)`, stripped of trailing `.:,`, and (ii) every capture of `\bafter row (\d+[a-z]?(?:\.[a-z0-9]+)?)\b` — which (i) cannot capture, because `row` holds no character from `[#:A-Z0-9]`. A type-(ii) reference resolves iff N is the first cell of an EARLIER §4 row. A type-(i) reference X resolves iff X equals the first cell of an earlier §4 row, or equals a TOKEN of an earlier row's BATCH cell — the maximal `[#A-Za-z0-9._-]+` runs of the cell after deleting `*` and `` ` ``, trailing `.` removed. Item cells are NOT read, and `row:N` is NOT read as `row N`. Earlier = before the referring row in document order.
- **Why this rule.** OBJ-2 says OBJ-9's rewrite exists so that *"census list (e) then needs no key table"*, and OBJ-9's verification is *"a recount at the ref finds no key reference left"*. Resolving through item cells IS a key table: the item cells of rows 32, 44 and 68 read `row:6`, `row:7`, `row:3h.b` — `order.py` WAVES keys — and row 107's item cell holds the id OBJ-2 moves into its batch cell. And `row:6` is not row 6: it is the key of row 32 (batch cell `plan row 6`; DL-A4's key-to-row map), so reading it as `row 6` resolves it to the wrong row ("Months of database headroom").
- **Result.** Type (i): **83 references in 45 rows, 35 unmatched**, identical at `9d80cd991` and `d4a2679c4` — 26 `ACCUMULATION-GATE` (rows 126-134, 136-147, 149-153 → row 125); row 100's `row:6`, `row:7`, `RESTING-ORDER-DEADLINE`, `row:3h.b`; 107a's `T-W20C-SCALAR-LEG`; 191's `PROCESS-DEATH-FORM`; 194's `KRAKEN-LIVE-KEY`; 195's `rm:25-16` (**34 `order.py` keys**) and 160a's `B-CREDENTIALS-PRIVATE-REPO` (a §0 plate, not a §4 row). All 35 name nothing; none names a later row. Type (ii): **10** at `d4a2679c4` (1a, 9a-9h, 183a), **6** at `9d80cd991`, all resolving.
- **Post-edit check** (simulated on a copy of the `d4a2679c4` plan): OBJ-2's one-time edits (CD-A13: rows 16, 107, 120, 148, 157), then 34 references rewritten to `after row N` — the 33 keys other than `T-W20C-SCALAR-LEG`, plus row 100's `F-G-1-REOPEN` → row 16 (PF-A15) — leave **1 of 93** unmatched: `B-CREDENTIALS-PRIVATE-REPO`, the scope's *"one real miss"*. `T-W20C-SCALAR-LEG` needs no rewrite: P4's row-107 edit resolves it.
- **Controls.** Positive: row 160a's `B-CREDENTIALS-PRIVATE-REPO` (the miss the scope names) is found; row 37's `after #506` resolves to row 36 through its batch cell. Negative, planted into row 2's note on a copy: `after B-SIZING-DEC-RESTORE` (row 9, later) → unmatched, later; `after B-NOT-A-REAL-BATCH` → unmatched; `after #628` (row 10, later) → unmatched, later. Sensitivity: adding item-cell resolution gives PF's 31; reading `row:N` as `row N` gives CE's 33.
- **Population.** The note cells of the 214 (`9d80cd991`) / 218 (`d4a2679c4`) §4 rows. ⚠️ **Residual, not a count difference:** 10 `#nnn` references (DL-A5: row 37 `#506`; row 100 `#628 #977 #972 #566 #204 #166 #527`; 135a `#221`; 179 `#517`) resolve today through `#nnn — batch named at Step 1` batch cells. CD-A13's naming form keeps the `#nnn` in parentheses, and a later naming edit that drops one of THESE ten is reported by list (e) the next Monday — not silently. ⚠️ **Reach limit found in round 1:** four more ordering references are captured by neither type. Rows 39a, 97a, 160a and 166a write `after` followed by a backticked `#nnn` (`#977`, `#561`, `#615`, `#692`), and `[#A-Za-z0-9:._-]` holds no backtick. Each resolves today only through a `#nnn — batch named at Step 1` batch cell (rows 39, 97, 160, 166), which are the cells OBJ-2/P5 overwrite when those batches are named. So for these four, a naming edit that drops the `#nnn` breaks the reference **silently**. An optional backtick around X gives 87 references / 35 unmatched at `d4a2679c4` (the four resolve). Row 179's `after #517 and B-SCHEDULER-FIRST-TICK` captures only its first target. Whether to widen the capture or rewrite the four is Question 22 (§1 row 41).

**R4 — row 16's F-G grammar** (a §4 row with no parseable batch-cell id; census list (d); OBJ-1 coverage). **Not decided here — the grammar is Question 7.**
- **Rule, both variants.** A §4 row as in R3. Its id is `extractLeadingBatchId` (`config.mjs:67-74` at the ref: the first pattern whose match starts at index 0) applied to the batch cell with `*` and `` ` `` deleted and leading whitespace trimmed. **NO-F-G:** `BATCH_ID_PATTERNS` with P31's B-* replacement and T-* addition. **F-G:** the same plus `/\bF-G-\d+[a-z]?\b/`.
- **Result.** NO-F-G: **116 of 214** at `9d80cd991`, **116 of 218** at `d4a2679c4` (the same under today's pattern — P31 changes no §4 batch cell's parse). F-G: **115 of 214**, **115 of 218**. The one row is 16. Gradable rows: 98 vs 99 of 214, 102 vs 103 of 218; after OBJ-2's edits (simulated, with P31) 106 vs 107 of 218. Census list (d) under CE P43's rule: **5 rows** NO-F-G (16 `REOPENED — Step 3`, 107, 120, 148, 157) vs **4 rows** F-G (107, 120, 148, 157).
- **Controls.** Positive: row 9a's `B-TRADE-LOSS-BOUND-DECISION (`#1105`)` parses under both; row 16 parses (as `F-G-1`) only under F-G. Negative: row 10 `#628 — batch named at Step 1`, row 32 `plan row 6` and row 107 `— batch named at Step 1` return null under both. Under F-G, `F-G-1-REOPEN` already parses as `F-G-1` (the pattern stops after the digit), so the rename matters for row 100's reference (R3) and for report-file matching, not for the parse.
- **Population.** The 214 / 218 §4 rows. Their no-id rows split 109 `batch named at Step 1`, 6 plan-row pointers (32, 44, 51, 68, 86, 100) and 1 other (16).
- **Which question decides it.** **Question 7** — whether `/\bF-G-\d+[a-z]?\b/` joins `BATCH_ID_PATTERNS`. The same ruling sets list (d)'s count and the census's HOME-id grammar (CE P43 assumes yes).

## 2. INCIDENT DURING THIS AUDIT

**DL-A0.** While measuring OBJ-5's regeneration delta, a reader ran a scratch copy of `plan_doc.py` whose `DEST` rewrite failed, so the script wrote into **CC-B's working tree**: `C:\DawnTraderV3-new\1-system-manual\SPRINT_TO_LIVE_PLAN.md`, at 2026-09-30 01:22:17 +02:00 (34 insertions and 59 deletions against CC-B's HEAD `2a7934768`; among other losses, the 20 lettered rows gone). Nothing was committed or pushed. The reader also created and then removed a temporary `/tmp/.x_cron` file on both servers during the cron census.
**Recovery (CC-A, 2026-09-30):** CC-A confirmed the overwritten file byte-identical to the generator output, backed it up, restored it to CC-B's HEAD `2a7934768` with `git checkout`, and told NEW Claude. When this document was written, `git status --porcelain` for that path in CC-B's clone read empty (CC-B has since committed further).
**What it proves:** it is a **live positive control for OBJ-5's premise** — run from ANY clone or scratch folder, these scripts write into CC-B's tree, because both `DEST`s are absolute paths into it. It goes into the `DELETED_COMPONENTS_LOG.md` entry as the measured instance of the hazard (P17).

## 4. HOW §5 IS LAID OUT

Each area carries, in this order: what the read covered; its **census** table (writers / readers / mutators / deleters / schedulers — a cell the input did not state reads "not stated by the input"); an **exactly-one / entry-point** note; its **findings**, each with its evidence and status (`established` unless marked — a finding whose status contains a HYPOTHESIS is tagged in bold); its **re-derived numbers**; its **ledger search**; its **SIM / System Manual** read; and its **risks**.

**Exactly one / entry points, as this area's census states them.** The plan has **exactly ONE code writer** (`plan_doc.py`, :8 → :143, deleted by OBJ-5) and **ZERO code readers** at the ref — the checker will be its first. The id pattern has **exactly TWO code readers**: `extractLeadingBatchId` at poller.mjs:51 (the grading path) and `extractBatchId` at checker.mjs:313 (backtest only). `decideOrphanSweep` has **a single call site** (tick :703-713). **One scheduler** over all of them: `governance-checker.timer` (30 min), fronted by the box-only `20-auto-redeploy.conf`, which makes a push a deploy. ⚠️ **Minor disagreement between inputs on what `state.json` persists:** CD says openAlerts, lastTick and gradedRefSha; CE (read live) lists openAlerts(4), lastTick, **fetchFailStreak** and gradedRefSha.

#### 5.1.b Findings

- **CD-A1** — The poller does NOT carry the lower bound Langston ruled (`lastCode ?? earliest leading-id commit`). There is no earliest-leading-id field. Worse, the `lastCode` that decideAlerts sees on a graded batch is not the code close. anchorClosedBatches overwrites it with the report's first-add for every closed, non-reopened batch, and grading runs only behind that same sentinel. Using `s.lastCode` as the bound would therefore make the report time the lower bound. Every Step-10 plan edit made before the Step-11 report would then read as a miss. Two new fields are needed, both set in computeBatchStates and never touched by the anchor: `firstLead` (earliest leading-id commit, any files) and `lastCodeRaw` (the unpinned code close). **Amended after fresh-reader round 1 — "never touched by the anchor" is also these fields' defect.** The anchor pins `lastCode` precisely because a later commit that merely leads with a closed id would otherwise move it (the `#350` vector; poller.mjs:90-99 comment and :106 at `158c66983`). `firstLead` and `lastCodeRaw` drop that pin, so a post-report leading commit moves the bound past the Step-10 edit it must credit. Measured shape: B-BALANCE-TRUTH — report and Step 10 in `e8eea2158` (2026-09-23T06:30:20Z), then a leading-id code commit `23f81527a` (server/storage.ts) 13 min later; an unpinned bound = `23f81527a` reads the Step-10 edit as a miss. Population: §1 row 22.1 (2 of 33 recent closes have a post-report leading code commit). The bound must be capped or filtered at the report's first-add; the form is a question for Langston (new, round 1), and P35/P36 carry it.
  - *Evidence:* poller.mjs:57 (the state object: firstCode, lastCode, hasGovernance, _files only); :61-64 (code-only min/max); :106 `b.lastCode = b.completionAddTime; // pin to the immutable close event`; :314 (grading is behind `s.hasCompletionReport`, which the anchor sets at :104)
  - *Status:* established
- **CD-A2** — The inclusive bound is required. A correct close that puts the code, the report and the plan edit in ONE commit has its only in-window leading commit equal to the bound. A strict `>` would open a false gov-planline on it. The measured case is B-REACH-BASELINE-ADJUST: 07c806722 (2026-09-29T13:40:20Z) converts the progress report to the completion report, touches scripts/inventory/plan_doc.py (so firstCode = lastCode = that commit) and edits its own §5 line (a − and + pair). The preview opens it under `>` and credits it under `>=`.
  - *Evidence:* `git show --stat 07c806722`: SPRINT_TO_LIVE_PLAN.md 4 +-, B_REACH_BASELINE_ADJUST_COMPLETION_REPORT.md +130, scripts/inventory/plan_doc.py 4 +-. preview.mjs at 9d80cd991 and at d4a2679c4, gate 2026-09-01: `lb=2026-09-29T13:40:20Z -> OPENS` with `>`; `CREDITED by 07c806722-,07c806722+` with `>=`.
  - *Status:* established
- **CD-A3** — Positive-control preview, pre-registered. At 9d80cd991 with the gate at 2026-09-01T00:00Z and the rule as specified (inclusive bound, whole-file adds excluded, content attribution): the tick grades four batches that have a report (five if F-G parsing is added). It OPENS gov-planline for B-XSTOCK-FEE-CONTRACT (§5 leg) and nothing else. B-XSTOCK-FEE-CONTRACT: report first-add 2026-09-12T00:59:24Z; bound = firstLead 77c5b7c35 at 2026-09-29T14:59:11Z (no code in the window); no own-line change at any time. It stays open under the widened `firstCode ?? firstLead` bound too. B-REACH-BASELINE-ADJUST is credited. B-GOV-REPORTING, B-ARCHIVE-RETENTION-SIZING (and F-G-2, only if F-G parsing is added) are graded but owe nothing: they sit on §0 only, which falls to the census by ruling. At d4a2679c4 with the same gate the result is identical. At d4a2679c4 with the gate at landing time (2026-09-30), NOTHING is graded: all five report first-adds (B-GOV-REPORTING 09-29T19:24Z, B-ARCHIVE-RETENTION-SIZING 09-29T14:11Z, B-XSTOCK-FEE-CONTRACT 09-12T00:59Z, B-REACH-BASELINE-ADJUST 09-29T13:40Z, F-G-2 09-28T21:27Z) predate the gate. So the live set at the flip is empty. **Amended after fresh-reader round 1 (re-derived at `158c66983` and `9d80cd991`):** (i) the OPEN is not a demonstrated close miss — B-XSTOCK-FEE-CONTRACT's report (`b6ade3d40`, 2026-09-12) predates the plan's birth (`d675900d4`, 2026-09-28) by 16 days, its only in-window leading commits are `77c5b7c35` (touching only its completion report) and `cb948417f`, and its bound sits 17 days after its report, an instance of A7's residual; it still opens under every bound form, because its §5 line has no M-change on the plan's path at any time. (ii) The preview grades **no §4 leg**: `git show 9d80cd991:<plan> | grep -nF <id>` finds none of the four graded ids in a §4 batch cell (B-GOV-REPORTING L11/L65/L78 note; B-ARCHIVE-RETENTION-SIZING L20/L83 note; B-XSTOCK-FEE-CONTRACT L19/L209 note/L345 §5; B-REACH-BASELINE-ADJUST L17/L344 §5). (iii) No graded close has the normal shape — a Step-10 row edit in a commit BEFORE the report commit; B-REACH-BASELINE-ADJUST's edit is inside its report commit `07c806722`. So OBJ-1's primary leg (§4) and the Step-10-then-report shape have **no historical positive control**; they rest on the P34/P36 unit tests and on the synthetic-fixture preview run added to P39. The approved scope calls B-XSTOCK-FEE-CONTRACT "the known own-line miss" — that departure is put to Langston (new question, round 1).
  - *Evidence:* `node preview.mjs 9d80cd991 2026-09-01T00:00:00Z` and `node preview_ge.mjs <ref> <gate>` (window d7d8d509d 2026-09-24T14:57Z → 9d80cd991; window 69c9d8d1b 2026-09-26T22:34Z → d4a2679c4). The preview replicates the tick's enrolment: the -n300 window, the leading id, the completion first-add via batchIdToFileRegex + /COMPLETION|COMPLETE/ and Math.min, scope Math.max for reopen, the anchor, and applyCutoff at the box's GOV_CUTOFF 2026-06-24T12:07:01Z (go-live.conf). It does NOT replicate na-skip suppression, the dedupe cache or the orphan sweep.
  - *Status:* established
- **CD-A4** — The Q2 residual (a Step-10 plan edit followed by a later code commit, which would read as a miss) does NOT appear among graded closes in the window at either ref. - **CD-A8** — The row parser, specified at the ref. In §4 (10 wave tables) the header line is exactly `| # | item | batch / reference | owner | status | report | note |` (plan lines 78, 109, 146, 208, 236, 272, 279, 293, 312, 335). A §4 ROW is a table line with EXACTLY 7 cells whose first cell matches /^\d+[a-z]?$/. Its BATCH CELL is cells[2] (the third column). In §5 the header is exactly `| item | owner | closes |` (line 346). A §5 ROW is a 3-cell table line whose first cell is not a session label (/^(CC-[ABC]|Infra Claude)\b/), not `item`, and not a separator. Its ITEM CELL is cells[0]. §0 (`| session | in flight now | disposition |`, line 9) and §6 (`| session | group | items |`, line 362) rows start with a session label and are never graded. The id in a cell is extractLeadingBatchId(cell with * and backticks stripped), so `B-TRADE-LOSS-BOUND-DECISION (`#1105`)`, `P19-B6.10` and `B-USER-RESOLUTION-PIN` parse, while `#628 — batch named at Step 1`, `plan row 6` and `roadmap 16.7 — …` return null. For a CHANGED line, one leading `+` or `-` is stripped. Only lines after a `@@` hunk header are content; `diff --git`, `index`, `---`, `+++`, `new file` and `\ No newline` lines are skipped. A changed line that is not a §4 or §5 row (prose, §0, §6, a header, a separator) neither credits nor requires anything. The shape rule was checked against section position: 0 mismatches at 9d80cd991 and at d4a2679c4. **Not at `158c66983` (fresh-reader round 1):** row 138a (plan:254) carries a stray `SUPERSEDED:` cell from `b5cd7cfd8` (2026-09-30) and has 8 cells (§4: 219 seven-cell + 1 eight-cell numbered rows), so under the 7-cell rule it is silently not a row — B-PATTERN-SIZE-CAP-REVIEW's leg would not be required, and census list (d) (same rule, R3/R4) cannot see it either. And because the §4 header occurs 10 times, "the §4 header line is not found" cannot fire when a column is added to ONE wave table: 9 headers still match and that wave's rows drop out. The parser must therefore (P33) report every §4-section line whose first cell matches /^\d+[a-z]?$/ but whose cell count is not 7 as `malformed`, and require EVERY §4 table header to match exactly; what the tick does with a malformed row is a question for Langston (new, round 1). The stray cell itself is a plan defect — §8, disposition 1. Fail-closed: if the plan is absent or empty at the ref, OR the §4 header line or the §5 header line is not found, the tick raises its own alert and grades nothing for this rule.
  - *Evidence:* plan.md at d4a2679c4, via `grep -nF` of the three header strings; preview.mjs reports `shape/position mismatches 0` at both refs
  - *Status:* established

#### 5.1.e SIM and System Manual for this area

SYSTEM_IMPACT_MAP at d4a2679c4 covers the checker's alerting-path writers (:910-916), the gov-ledgerrow precedent under Session Task Lists (:1066) and the #637 cross-process state contract (:3609-3613; the scope's :3604-3608 has drifted by +5). It is SILENT on the sprint plan as a component (SPRINT_TO_LIVE / PRE_LIVE_SPRINT: 0 lines; control PHASE_19_PLAN: 3) and SILENT on the batch-id parser (BATCH_ID_PATTERNS / extractLeadingBatchId: 0; control governance-checker: 10). OBJ-10's SIM entry should therefore name, in addition: the plan as a checker-read artefact (path, the two header lines, the parse rule), the gov-planline and gov-planline-unreadable keys, the plan_line na-skip value, the new firstLead/lastCodeRaw batch-state fields, and the exported planRowsByBatch join as B-SLOT-PLACEMENT-CHECK's input. SYSTEM_MANUAL is silent on the checker (0 lines; control PHASE_19_PLAN: 4). That is consistent with the scope's N/A for SYSTEM_MANUAL; nothing in OBJ-1/OBJ-2 is architecture, strategy, regime, filter, pipeline or maths.

#### 5.1.f Risks named by this area

1. Push = deploy. The auto-redeploy drop-in fast-forwards the checker clone before each tick, so the code is live within about 30 minutes. It must land with `enabled:false` in committed source. The box's go-live.conf sets GOV_SHADOW=0, so once flipped, every gov-planline alert goes straight to the real queue (shadow mode is global, config.mjs:228).
2. The lower bound is window-scoped (A7), and as first drafted it is UNPINNED (A1's amendment: a post-report leading commit moves it; 2 of 33 recent closes) — to be capped at the report's first-add, form per the new bound question: about 3 days of window at the current commit rate. A close whose last code has left the window can false-alert.
3. (Widened after fresh-reader round 1: ANY note-only or homing edit to a batch's row after its bound pays its close — 26 of 28 −/+ row pairs in the plan's history left status and report unchanged; A14's amendment, Question 8 option (d).) The inclusive bound lets a code-less batch's own Step-1 row edit pay its close (A14). The OBJ-2 commit itself can pay in-flight F-G-1's close if F-G parsing is added.
4. (Corrected after fresh-reader round 1: as first specified, a column added to ONE of the ten §4 wave tables, or a stray cell in one row — row 138a at `158c66983` — was a SILENT pass, not an alert; P33 now reports malformed rows and requires every §4 header, A8's amendment.) The plan layout is load-bearing: the rule fails closed on a missing header line. A future column added to §4 or §5 turns into an alert, not a silent pass. Intended, but it will page.
5. The single per-batch key freezes the alert body.
6. The pattern fix changes attribution for 64 historical commits; 0 are in today's window. B-RULES-1c/1d still cannot see their combined report, which is latent.
7. Adding F-G parsing mints about 5 alerts on the first tick (A11).
8. The checker's tests run in no CI until OBJ-10's CI job lands. Land that job before or with this code.
9. tick() has no test harness, so the wiring (plan read, fail-closed, orphan verifier) is covered only through the pure units.

#### 5.2.b Findings

- **CE-A1** — The Discord post does NOT start with the alert body. It starts with a category header, then the bold title, then the body, then the category/id footer and a metadata slice. A re-surface prepends further text to both the title and the body. So no body can make a post 'lead with' a name.

Metadata is JSON.stringify(metadata).slice(0,300) at :87. The cut is at :102. frameResurface :177-178 prefixes the title with '⏰ RE-SURFACE #n — STILL UNRESOLVED:' and the body with 'owned by …/UNCLAIMED …, open ~Nh.'
  - *Status:* established
- **CE-A2** — The census reaches Langston with no change anywhere: his bridge engages every post from the alerts webhook, whatever its text. Leading the body with 'Langston' is harmless and has no mechanical effect. ADDRESS_START_RE gates only CC-bot posts, and the post starts with the header anyway (A1).
  - *Evidence:* Live discord-langston-bridge.py :726-727: is_alert = (wh_id is not None and CFG alerts_webhook_id … and wh_id == CFG['alerts_webhook_id']). :784-785: 'if is_alert: pass # … always engage (OBJ-5), bypass the name gate'. :793: the CC-bot path needs ADDRESS_START_RE.match(content). Repo copy has the same predicate at :750-751.
  - *Status:* established
- **CE-A3** — The OBJ-4 routing condition ('wakes CC-A and not Langston') cannot be met by any governance-category alert without a change outside the checker. (1) The CC bridge never logs a non-Kyle message, so the alert post's text never reaches the wake filter. (2) The wake filter has no branch for the kind Langston's bridge writes when an alert arrives. (3) Langston's bridge engages every alerts-webhook post (A2), so he is always invoked. CC-A is woken only if Langston's triage reply names CC-A in prose; the owner marker alone stopped waking at #995 OBJ-11. Otherwise CC-A sees the row only at its next turn, through inject-due-alerts.
  - *Evidence:* Live discord-cc-bridge.py :164-165: 'if message.author.id != CFG["kyle_id"]: return'. cc-wake-filter.py :304-486 handles kinds '', voice_inbound, langston_outbound, langston_outbound_media and cc_outbound, and there is no langston_alert_inbound branch. Population: all of /var/log/cc-discord-inbox.jsonl on Helsinki, measured now, 23,941 lines with 1,728 langston_alert_inbound rows; positive control: the langston_outbound branch exists and there are 5,616 such rows. Marker no longer wakes: cc-wake-filter.py :407-421. Measured over the same log: of 307 governance alert posts, 187 got a reply whose last marker was owner=CC-A, and 144 of those 187 (77%) name CC-A in prose after stripping markers, so they would wake CC-A under the current filter. Instrument positive control: 144 > 0. Limit: this is the current filter replayed over history, not a record of actual wakes.

- **CE-A4** — A non-delivered category DOES exist at the held ref. `verification` at `info` severity is never posted to Discord, so Langston is not engaged. The row still reaches every session's per-turn read (inject-due-alerts filters on state and due time, not category). This is how 8a07c40b was built. Its failure was that no successor row was ever minted and the ack removed it from the read. Delivery did not fail. A per-ISO-week key minted by the clock removes that failure: acking week N cannot stop week N+1.

The checker hard-codes governance at poller.mjs:410, so the category must become a parameter of alertSink.add (checker-only change, not under the hold).

  - *Fresh-reader round 1 (§1 FR1-CE-4):* "so Langston is not engaged" holds for his bridge only. His own per-turn §10.5 read has no category or severity filter, so he surfaces and triages a `verification`/`info` row on every invocation until CC-A acks it.

His weekly census triage will therefore by default assign follow-through to CC-A, and name CC-A in prose most of the time (A3), which wakes CC-A every week for Langston's own row.

- **CE-A6** — Where the gate goes. tick() has three exits past loadState. Fetch-fail (:550-573) returns before any ref read, so the census must not run there; a catch-up gate retries on the next tick. Rulebook-unreadable (:634-649) returns after a good fetch, and the census does not need the exception ledger. The normal end is :715-719. The census must be called at both post-fetch exits: in the :645 branch before its saveState, and after the orphan sweep at ~:714 before the final saveState. It must be wrapped in its own try/catch and use the liveIds snapshot already taken at :589.
  - *Evidence:* poller.mjs :545-573 (fetch-fail: state.gradedRefSha=null; lastTick; saveState; return), :579-581 (gradedRefSha set), :589-590 (liveIds snapshot and prune, taken before this tick's adds), :634-649 (rulebook throw path: saveState; return {rulebookUnreadable:true}), :715-719 (end).
  - *Status:* established
- **CE-A7** — A crash after the census add can re-mint the census, because state is persisted only at a tick's exit. An uncaught throw later in the tick (decideAlerts, the orphan sweep, a sink add throwing — runCli uses execFileSync, which throws on a non-zero exit) loses the in-memory lastCensusWeek. The next tick would then add a second row for the same week. The gate is the sole dedupe (condition 1) and the key is per-week in openAlerts only, so nothing else stops the second add. Fix by construction: call saveState(state) immediately after a successful census add.
  - *Evidence:* saveState call sites :571, :647, :718 only. alertSink.add :412 calls runCli with no catch (:368-372 execFileSync). tick() has no top-level try (:536-720).
  - *Status:* established (mechanism); no incidence (the census does not exist yet)
- **CE-A8** — The ISO-week gate works as the sole dedupe, and the openAlerts table must not be the dedupe. The per-tick prune drops a cached key as soon as the row is resolved, so a Monday resolve followed by a same-Monday tick would re-mint through a table-based dedupe. Census keys are otherwise inert in the table: the orphan sweep ignores unknown key types, and decideAlerts never emits them. So a gov-plancensus:<week> entry persists exactly until someone resolves the row. That makes it a valid signal for 'the previous week is still unresolved'.
  - *Evidence:* decideStaleOpenAlertDrops :210-213 (drops ids not in liveIds). liveAlertIds :441 (active, acknowledged, scheduled). decideOrphanSweep :194 ('other orphan key types are not swept here'). Dedupe by openAlerts :672-677.

#### 5.2.f Risks named by this area

Push is deploy: census code reaches the box within about 30 minutes of the push, outside dt-deploy and before Step-4 completes, so the flags must default off in committed source (P1). A crash after the add re-mints the week unless state is saved at the add (A7). The body cap is load-bearing: an overrun removes the metadata slice (where the counts show) first, and the alert id only after about 315 more characters (A18, corrected at §1 FR1-CE-6). Every list rests on prose conventions in RUNNING_ISSUES and the plan; one leading parenthetical moved a count by 1, the HOME-id reading moves placement by 18-19 on one grammar, the Question-7 grammar moves it by 2, and dropping note-cell matches would move it by 11 (§1 FR1-CE-1 to FR1-CE-3; the earlier "up to 33" mixed two scripts). The census must print its own parse stats and refuse on zero heads, and a rule change must go through Langston. The weekly census engages Langston and, through his triage, usually CC-A (A5). The mistake pass under governance engages Langston against his own condition (A3). Time budget: the census adds about 5 git reads (RUNNING_ISSUES is 3.1 MB) to a tick measured at about 11 s against TimeoutStartSec=120. That is not measured on the box yet, so the dry run must time it. Heartbeat changes deploy only through the poller's fast-forward of the shared clone; if that fast-forward fails, the heartbeat runs stale code, which the drift-list change (P10) now flags. The heartbeat has no tests today, so P9's refactor is the first coverage and must not change the silent-poller path.

- **DL-A12** — OBJ-5's approved verification cannot pass as worded. 'A whole-tree grep for either governed path returning nothing' will match the rule-18 .removed archive copies the same objective requires: plan_doc.py.removed will contain line 8's DEST and sort.py.removed line 8's. It will also match the prose that quotes the paths (this batch's scope :44 quotes the plan_doc.py:8 DEST). The verification needs a stated population. Proposed: `git grep -n -E 'DEST *=|io\.open\(DEST' <ref> -- scripts ':!1-system-manual/_archive'` returns nothing at the new ref, and the same command at 9d80cd991 returns plan_doc.py:8/:143 and sort.py:8/:181 as the control. Add a second check that the four paths are absent from `git ls-tree -r <ref> scripts/inventory/`.

  - *Status:* ESTABLISHED (the archive copy will carry the string); the replacement command is a PROPOSAL
  - *Fresh-reader round 1 (re-derived at `158c66983`, `d4a2679c4`, `9d80cd991`): THE PROPOSAL ABOVE FAILS TOO.* The `DEST *=|io\.open\(DEST` grep over `scripts` returns **10** lines at all three refs, not 4: the four named, plus `scripts/analysis/lc5-preinstall-exercise.py:42`, `scripts/codex-export/build-codex-repo.mjs:45`, `scripts/inventory/draft.py:5`/`:308` and `render.py:5`/`:67`. After the deletions **6** remain, all in files the ruling leaves (§10c: *"nothing else in `scripts/inventory/` touched"*). So it cannot pass, and its control was misreported. `':!1-system-manual/_archive'` does nothing, because the pathspec is already `scripts`. Also, the write lines `:143`/`:181` read `io.open(DEST, "w", …)` and contain no path, so no grep for a governed path can return them. That was already true of the scope's own control. **Replacement:** (1) the four paths are absent from `git ls-tree -r <ref> scripts/inventory/`, which covers the write lines; (2) `git grep -n -F -e 'DawnTraderV3-new\1-system-manual\SPRINT_TO_LIVE_PLAN' -e 'DawnTraderV3-new\Claude Comms and Packages\Scope Files\PRE_LIVE_SPRINT' <ref> -- ':!*.md' ':!1-system-manual/_archive'` returns nothing. **Control:** at `158c66983` and at `9d80cd991`, (2) returns exactly `plan_doc.py:8` and `sort.py:8`. Without the `*.md` exclusion it also returns the scope's :44 and four lines of this document, which is the prose collision this finding names. The `.removed` copies are under `_archive` and are excluded. The survivors `draft.py`/`render.py` are DL-A15.

#### 5.3.f Risks named by this area

This audit broke the no-write rule twice. First, an unguarded run of the generator overwrote CC-B's working-tree plan file (A0); nothing was committed, and a lossless restore is named. Second, a transient /tmp/.x_cron file was created and then removed on both servers. Batch risks: (1) Ordering. If the working order or order.py is deleted before OBJ-9's rewrite and the Wave 0 citation carry-over land, 34 plan references lose their only in-tree key and the one PLS-ahead correction is lost. Git history and the .removed copy still hold both, but the pre-audit table removes the dependency. (2) OBJ-5's verification as worded fails against its own archive copies (A12). (3) Removing the working order drops the only per-item category tags. (4) Three sessions edit PRE_LIVE_SPRINT.md and the plan concurrently: 14 PLS commits since 09-29, mostly in the after-live region. The deletion diff removes 241 lines, so it must be rebased on the latest pull, and the recounts printed in the commit message must be taken at the commit's own ref. (5) Until the deletion lands, any session that runs plan_doc.py or sort.py from any folder silently overwrites CC-B's working copy, as measured in A0. *(Fresh-reader r1:)* The deletion does NOT end this hazard class. `draft.py` and `render.py`, which the ruling leaves, write the same way into CC-B's clone (DL-A15; Question 34).

#### 5.4.b Findings

- **PF-A1** — The OPEN-issue parse reproduces the Step-1 baseline counts for entry heads and distinct numbers exactly. It lands one below the reader's OPEN figure. Rule: a HEAD is a line matching `^(?:#{2,4} |- \*\*|\*\*)#(\d+)\b(.*)$`. The state is the remainder after stripping leading whitespace, `*` and the markers ✅ ★ ⛔ ⚠️, upper-cased. An issue is OPEN if some head's state startswith OPEN and no head's state startswith CLOSED, RESOLVED, WITHDRAWN, DONE or FIXED. At 9d80cd991 this gives 766 heads, 663 distinct numbers and 468 OPEN (reader: 469). At d4a2679c4 it gives 770 heads, 667 numbers and 471 OPEN. Stripping ✅ matters: without it #482/#488/#489/#495/#496 ('✅ RESOLVED … by B-LANGSTON-QUEUE-2') count as open, which gives 473. Reach limit: #532 has a single head 'CORE DEFECT CLOSED 2026-07-22 (OBJ-1), ISSUE REMAINS OPEN …' (RUNNING_ISSUES.md:315), so it is classed not-OPEN although it is open.
  - *Evidence:* Script parse2.py run on `git show 9d80cd991:…/RUNNING_ISSUES.md` and on the ref copy. Output: 'True heads 766 distinct 663 open 468' and 'True heads 770 distinct 667 open 471'. The diff between the emoji-strip and no-strip variants is exactly #482, #488, #489, #495, #496, each carrying a '✅ RESOLVED 2026-07-11' head (RI lines 513, 486, 482, 441, 431). Case-sensitive and alternative head-pattern variants all give 468.
  - *Status:* established (counts); the one-issue gap to the reader's 469 is unexplained
- **PF-A2** — OBJ-8 parse rule, written out: a DATED HOME is a line inside an OPEN issue's span that contains the word `HOME` (`\bHOME\b`) and a match of `\bdue\b\W{0,3}(20\d\d-\d\d-\d\d)`, case-insensitive. A match is excluded as QUOTED-STRUCK when it is immediately preceded by `"`, `(`` ` or `` ` ``. An issue's span runs from each of its heads to the line before the next head or the next `#`/`##` heading. At the ref this gives 41 matches on 39 lines across 30 OPEN issues, and the same 30 at 9d80cd991. The reader's 24 is reproduced as the 23 issues where a lowercase `due` follows HOME on the line, plus #738. The six extra issues are #444 (`DUE:`), #480, #481, #484 and #486 (`Due`/`due` before HOME on the line) and #682 (`DUE:`). Positive controls: #738 L4465 'DUE 2026-09-05' is found (upper case), and #705 L3714 is found. Negative controls: the three quoted-struck matches are excluded — #744 L4376 ('this entry READ "DUE 2026-09-05"', a date struck under §9.4), #736 L4505 ('This line read "DUE 2026-09-11…', since PLACED) and #705 L3723 (a status note quoting the date). Also excluded: #907's due line L5061, because #907 has a WITHDRAWN head (L5079), and #906 (WITHDRAWN L5078, CLOSED L6422).
  - *Evidence:* dated.py / dated2.py run on RUNNING_ISSUES.md at d4a2679c4. Output: 'matches 44 live 41 lines 39 issues 30', '09-05 live lines 12 issues 11', and 'struck: [(705, 3723), (736, 4505), (744, 4376)]'. The reader-variant regex `HOME[^\n]{0,200}?\bdue\b\W{0,3}20\d\d-\d\d-\d\d` (case-sensitive) gives 23: [443, 448, 449, 452, 453, 455, 483, 485, 487, 492, 493, 494, 696, 705, 742, 743, 900, 901, 902, 903, 908, 909, 910].

#### 5.4.f Risks named by this area

Four risks carry into the plan items above:

1. **Blind census list (e).** If it reuses order.py's pattern, OBJ-9's 'after row N' rewrite makes it silent for all 30 converted references, a detector that cannot fire (A14).
2. **Renames that break references.** OBJ-2's rename of row 16 (F-G-1-REOPEN → F-G-1) breaks row 100's resolvable reference unless both land in one commit (A15). Re-pointing rows 35, 55 and 87 moves ids out of OBJ-1's permanent-exemption set, so the OBJ-1 preview must run after these edits, or its expected-open set will differ (A16).
3. **Plan collisions.** The plan changes many times a day (52 commits in about 2 days). Line-number before/after figures must be re-derived at the commit's own parent, not at d4a2679c4. The §6 recount, report cells and row count will move with every new row.
4. **Wrong-owner edits.** OBJ-8's CC-A edits touch a dense one-line-per-head region (RI:424-548) where several sessions' addenda interleave. Rule 25.c applies: read the staged hunk, because a matching path does not prove matching content.

A fifth point is not a defect: the 4 governed-doc citations all still resolve today, so the value of condition 5 is preventive. The 2 code citations show drift is real (2426→2423 and 4519→4581 inside a day).

- **HY-A23** *(added in fresh-reader round 1)* — The drift guard is fail-open in a way that condition 2's list extension can trip. `checkerCodeDrift` (poller.mjs:521-534) rev-parses every file in DRIFT_LOADED_FILES, at HEAD and at the branch, inside one try, and any throw returns `{ drifted: false, error }`. The tick (:593-606) never reads `drift.error`, and on `!drifted` it resolves any open `gov-code-drift` alert. So one listed file that is missing at either ref blinds the guard for every file and closes a live drift alert. This has two consequences. (i) Listing `census.mjs` before the commit that creates it (P29 in Group 5, P43 in Group 7) disables drift detection from that push until P43 lands. (ii) Even in the right order, when a newly listed file is pushed and the box's fast-forward fails, HEAD lacks the file while the branch has it. That is the drift the guard exists to catch, and it reads as clean. The fail-open is deliberate: the comment at :505-520 records Langston's Step-4 ruling of 2026-07-11, 'a git hiccup can't disable the enforcer'. Separately, `heartbeat-check.test.mjs`, which P30's heartbeat CI step runs, does not exist at the ref. The only test file in the checker directory is poller.test.mjs, so that step fails until P28 creates the file.
  - *Evidence:* poller.mjs:505-534 and :593-606 at 158c66983; `git ls-tree --name-only 158c66983 scripts/governance-checker/` lists 12 files, of which poller.test.mjs is the only test.
  - *Status:* established

#### 5.5.f Risks named by this area

- **The exceptions-parser change is live on push.** It is not behind OBJ-1's flag, and the box fast-forwards within about 30 minutes. If the parser lands without the P26 data edits, the strict shape drops B-DIAG-387's override and the confirmer rule flags L40, L42, L43, L45 and L53. So the parser and the data edits must share one commit, and the preview must be re-run at the push ref. Other sessions append open rows almost daily; every current form is `cc-c (…)`, `cc-b (…)` or `langston…`, and all of them pass.
- **Retyping other owners' rows** (L42, L43, L45, L111; and L51 if hygiene is taken) touches rows other sessions wrote. Tell the owners, or ask them to do it.
- **Heartbeat seam refactor:** heartbeat-check.mjs is outside DRIFT_LOADED_FILES until condition 2 lands, so a failed fast-forward would leave the box running old heartbeat code silently. Condition 2 and this refactor should ship together. `census.mjs` joins the list only in the commit that creates it (HY-A23).
- **Hollowness text:** narrowing the alert text to 'absent' is honest but makes the residual visible. It needs its home (Question 32) in the same turn.
- **CI:** the new steps must not run before vitest, or a checker failure hides the vitest result. Hence `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}` after 'Run tests'.
- **workflow-10:** its :124 and :130 line-number citations move with poller.mjs edits, and `:698` already moves in the P21-P26 commit. P52 converts them to symbols in that first poller.mjs commit; otherwise the always-loaded skill cites the wrong lines.
- **State file:** new state.json fields (lastCensusWeek, lastCensusAt) change the #637 cross-process contract. The heartbeat must treat a missing field before the first census as defined behaviour, not as stale forever.

## 6. THE IMPLEMENTATION PLAN

**Every item names the findings it falls out of. Items with no finding behind them are tagged `UNAUDITED`.** Each item sourced from a Step-2 read carries that read's text verbatim (its what / files and lines / tests / falls-out-of, with finding ids prefixed by area); this document adds only the P-number, the objective, and the ordering notes. Where two reads proposed the same change, the item is MERGED and both texts are carried.

**Dependency order (why the groups run in this sequence):**
1. **Carry before cutting** — the key-to-row mapping (DL-A4) and the reconcile table (DL-A6) live in this document (P2, P3) before anything is deleted.
2. **Correct the plan before deleting its generator and before the preview** — the after-rewrite (P6) and the Wave 0 citation carry (P7(a)) land in or before the deletion commits (P17, P20); the OBJ-2 pass lands (P4) before the flip (P61; CD-A14). Rows 35 and 87 are re-pointed (P12) when CC-C answers Question 26, and P39's third run reads P12's own commit. P12 gates neither the preview's pre-registered runs nor the flip (§1 row 43).
3. **The exceptions parser and its data edits ship in ONE commit** (P21-P26) — they are live on push.
4. **Tests into CI before or with new checker logic** (P30's poller step; its heartbeat step lands with or after P28, which creates the test file). The heartbeat seam and `heartbeat-check.mjs`'s entry in the drift list ship together (P28-P29), and `census.mjs` joins the drift list only in P43's commit (HY-A23); the dormant flags are in committed source, default OFF, before the code that reads them (P27, P32).
5. **All new checker behaviour lands dormant** (P31-P46, except the id-pattern fix P31, which is live on push and re-attributes 0 of the current 300 commits for its B-/T- half — 8 of 300 if the F-G line is ruled in, Question 7; P38's plan read and its `gov-planline-unreadable` alert are gated on `PLAN_LINE.enabled`).
6. **Flips last** — each by a committed one-line change after Langston's Step 4 and the committed preview / dry run (P61, P62); workflow-10's :119/:140 text lands in the OBJ-1 flip commit, and its :124/:130 line-to-symbol conversion lands with the first poller.mjs commit, P21-P26 (P52); `8a07c40b` is resolved only after the first `gov-mistakepass` row exists (P63).

> **Fresh-reader round 1, notes on the OBJ-10 items (P21-P30, P47-P55).**

The P-numbers map as P1→P21, P2→P22, P3→P23, P4→P24, P5→P25, P6→P26, P7→P47, P8→P48, P9→P28, P10→P30, P11→P50, P12→P52, P13→P53, P14→P55. The texts carried from other reads are not renumbered here (§1 row 40).
> - **Live on push is live BEFORE Step 4.** The box fast-forwards before every tick with `GOV_SHADOW=0`, and Step 4 runs at the graded ref after the push (`CLAUDE.md` §5 rule 9). So P21-P26, like P28-P29 and P31, grade live before Langston has reviewed them. Q9's committed flag exists to prevent that outcome for OBJ-1. It is put to Langston as a departure (round-1 question), and the options are named there. Until he rules, this group is not described as reviewed-before-live (§1 row 46).

### 6.7 Group 7 — OBJ-3 and OBJ-4 checker code (DORMANT: `WEEKLY_CENSUS_ENABLED = MISTAKE_PASS_ENABLED = false`)

**P40** · OBJ-3 condition 1 (sole dedupe) · *source: `s2_4.json` plan item [1]*  
- **What changes:** The weekly gate. A new function maybeRunWeekly(state, nowMs, liveIds) with its own try/catch, called at two sites: inside the rulebook-unreadable branch before its saveState (:645), and after the orphan sweep before the final saveState (~:714). It is not called on the fetch-fail path. It fires when isoWeek(now) !== state.lastCensusWeek AND now ≥ Monday 09:00Z of that week. That is the SOLE dedupe (condition 1), and it catches up on a later day if Monday's ticks all fail. On a successful add, or a logged shadow add, it sets lastCensusWeek, lastCensusAt and censusEnabledSince (first enabled tick) and calls saveState(state) IMMEDIATELY. On a thrown read or parse error it opens a fixed-key gov-census-failed (warning) through the openAlerts table, leaves the week unset so the next tick retries, and resolves that alert on the next success. The mistake pass uses the same gate with its own state keys: lastMistakePassWeek, so a census fault never blocks the pass and vice versa.
- **Files and lines:** scripts/governance-checker/poller.mjs :536-720 (tick), new helper beside it. State contract documented in SIM's #637 block (:3604-3617).
- **Tests / verification:** Two simulated Mondays → two distinct adds. A non-Monday tick in a week already run → none. A first tick on Tuesday of a week not yet run → one add (catch-up; put to Langston, Q3). resolve-then-tick on the same Monday → no second add. A throw after the add (simulated) → the next tick does not re-add (state saved at add). A census throw → gov-census-failed opened, enforcement results unchanged, the week not recorded. Shadow → logged once per week, not every tick.
- **falls out of:** CE-A6, CE-A7, CE-A8, CE-A23
- **Ordering / notes (this document):** Catch-up vs strict Monday is Question 10. **Corrected at fresh-reader round 1 (§1 FR1-CE-8):** `censusEnabledSince` and `mistakePassEnabledSince` are set on the FIRST tick that reads each flag on — at the top of `maybeRunWeekly`, before the gate, whether or not an add follows — and persisted by that tick's `saveState`; they are not written together with `lastCensusAt`. Otherwise an enabled census that never succeeds (a gate that never matches, with no throw to open `gov-census-failed`) leaves both keys null and P28's liveness never fires. The same edit stores `lastCensusRef` (the ref graded) beside `lastCensusWeek`/`lastCensusAt`, for list (a)'s window (P43 note). Added test: flag on for 9 days, no census ever run → P28 raises one warning. *Falls out of (correction):* CE-A6, CE-A13; §1 FR1-CE-8, FR1-CE-9.

**P43** · OBJ-3 lists (a)-(f) + OBJ-8 list (c) · *source: `s2_4.json` plan item [4]*  
- **What changes:** Census parse rules, fixed in code (a new module, census.mjs, pure with injected readers) and written into the pre-audit verbatim. All reads use `git show <BRANCH>:<path>` after a good fetch, and a null or empty read REFUSES. Sources: 1-system-manual/RUNNING_ISSUES.md, 1-system-manual/SPRINT_TO_LIVE_PLAN.md, 'Claude Comms and Packages/Scope Files/PRE_LIVE_SPRINT.md', 1-system-manual/POST_AUDIT_ROADMAP.md, and the ls-tree of Batch Completion/. LEDGER: an entry head is a line matching ^(### |- \*\*|\*\*)#(\d+). The status word is the first letter-run of the head text after repeatedly stripping leading non-letters and leading '(…)' groups. An issue is OPEN iff some head's word is OPEN and no head's word is in {CLOSED, RESOLVED, WITHDRAWN, DONE, FIXED}. An issue's text is the union of its heads' blocks (head to the next head). A HOME line is a line in that text containing the uppercase token \bHOME\b. HOME ids are every batch id on a HOME line, per the OBJ-2 BATCH_ID_PATTERNS (T-*/F-G-*/B-RULES-1e fixed); bare 'B-GOV' and fakes do not match. The id match is token-bounded: no letter, digit or hyphen before it; no letter, digit, underscore, '-<alnum>' or '.<digit>' after it. PLAN: the §4 rows are table rows ^\|\s*\d+[a-z]?\s*\| between '## 4.' and '## 5.' (cells: # | item | batch | owner | status | report | note). The §0 rows are table rows between '## 0.' and '## 1.' (session | in flight | disposition). The §5 rows lie between '## 5.' and '## 6.' (item | owner | closes). A line is CLOSED iff its status cell (§4), its in-flight or disposition cell (§0), or its closes cell (§5) matches (?<!NOT )\b(DONE|CLOSED)\b (case-insensitive). AFTER-LIVE: the '- ' lines under '## After live' plus '## Parked by Kyle' in PRE_LIVE_SPRINT.md (the parked list counts as placed; Q10). ROADMAP-POSITIONED (Q5): a POST_AUDIT_ROADMAP table row whose first cell is an item id ^\|\s*\d{1,2}([-.]\d+[a-z]?)+\s*\|, and that carries #N, OR a HOME line citing roadmap/POST_AUDIT_ROADMAP plus such an item id that exists as a row. A prose mention is not placement. LISTS: (a) completion-named files (the checker's findGlobDoc semantics) first-added (--diff-filter=A) since lastCensusAt, each matched with batchIdToFileRegex against the ids in §4 batch cells, §0 in-flight cells and §5 item cells, reported as not-closed-in-plan or in-no-plan-line. (b) OPEN issues where #N is in no §4 row, after-live line or parked line, and no HOME id is in them, and the issue is not roadmap-positioned. Placed-by-roadmap is shown as its own sub-count. The unplaced are diagnosed in precedence order: no HOME line; a HOME id has a completion-named file; HOME cites the roadmap in prose only; HOME cites PHASE_19_PLAN or a row with no id; HOME id in no list; HOME line with no id. (c) OPEN issues with a HOME line matching \bdue\s+\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2} (case-insensitive). A window-length date such as '+48h gate' is not caught. (d) a §4 row whose batch cell has no id AND (its status is not QUEUED, OR its item cell is exactly one batch id, OR a scope file's header 'Placement:' names that row). (e) in §4 note cells, `after X` (order.py's own pattern) where X is neither 'row N' naming an earlier row, nor an id in an earlier row's batch cell, nor a #N in an earlier row's batch cell. (f) closed-less §4/§0/§5 lines whose id has a completion-named file, reported as NEW vs state.census.prevF (the prior week's set, stored in state; condition 3), with the standing total shown as a count only. The census also prints a self-check line (heads/numbers/OPEN) and REFUSES on zero heads.
- **Files and lines:** new scripts/governance-checker/census.mjs; reuses checker.mjs findGlobDoc :96-102, firstAddCommitMs :130-139, config.mjs batchIdToFileRegex :79-87 and BATCH_ID_PATTERNS :13-23
- **Tests / verification:** A fixture per list with a positive and a negative control. (b): a head '(RENUMBERED …) OPEN' is OPEN; '#415 — [...]' is neither; a fake id and a bare prefix are unplaced; a parked-list mention is placed. (c): 'DUE 2026-09-05' is caught, '+48h gate' is not. (d): the row-107 shape is caught. (e): 'after row 12' earlier is OK, later is bad; 'after B-X' in no row is bad. (f): an unchanged set gives NEW=0, and one added line gives NEW=1.
- **falls out of:** CE-A14, CE-A15, CE-A16, CE-A17
- **Ordering / notes (this document):** **Settled (§1.1):** the OPEN rule here is **R1** (it subsumes PF-A1's emoji strip). **List (c)'s regex is replaced by R2** (`\bdue\b\W{0,3}<date>`, quoted-struck excluded). **List (e)'s resolution rule is replaced by R3** — no item-cell resolution, `row:N` is NOT `row N`, and an `after row N` capture is added. **List (d)** is 5 rows without an F-G pattern (16, 107, 120, 148, 157) and 4 with it (R4; Question 7) — the "F-G-* fixed" in this item's grammar is that ruling, not a given. Still open: the HOME-id reading moves placement across 321-354 — Question 9(b). `#559`'s double use (PF-A17) and `#532`-shaped heads (PF-A1) are reach limits (Questions 28, 27). **Fresh-reader round 1 corrections:** (1) **List (a) is windowed by REF, not clock** (§1 FR1-CE-9): list (a) is the top-level completion-named files with status `A` in `git diff --no-renames --name-status <lastCensusRef> <ref> -- 'Claude Comms and Packages/Batch Completion/'` (`lastCensusRef` stored by P40). The commit-time window would miss a report committed before a census tick and pushed after it (`checker.mjs:130-139` reads `%cI`). Added test: a report whose commit time predates the previous census, but which first appears between the two refs, is listed. The first-fire window (no `lastCensusRef` yet) is a new question. (2) **The `#N` match reads §4 note cells** (39 note-only matches at `9d80cd991`, mostly the plan's own "+ `#N`" homing form, §1 FR1-CE-3); whether an incidental note mention also places an issue is a new question, ruled before the P44 dry run. (3) **HOME ids use the grammar `config.mjs` actually carries** after P31 (plus F-G only if Question 7 is yes), imported, not re-implemented — §5.2's figures used a scratch grammar (§1 FR1-CE-1). (4) This commit also adds `census.mjs` to `DRIFT_LOADED_FILES` (`poller.mjs:521`) (P29 note, §1 FR1-CE-7). *Falls out of (corrections):* CE-A12, CE-A14, CE-A17; §1 FR1-CE-1, FR1-CE-3, FR1-CE-7, FR1-CE-9. **Round 1 adds two:** list (e) does not capture a backticked target (`after` + a backticked `#nnn`: rows 39a, 97a, 160a, 166a) or a second conjunctive target (row 179); the fixture set adds a backticked case (§1 row 41; Question 22). List (c) under R2 does not see an `OWNER … DUE` date on a line without `HOME` (`#680`, `#681`, `#684`; §1 row 40).

**P45** · OBJ-3 body · *source: `s2_4.json` plan item [6]*  
- **What changes:** Census body and where the lists live. Title: 'Weekly plan census <week>: sprint plan vs ledger (owner Langston)', 80 characters or fewer. The body is built to 1,000 characters or fewer (CENSUS_BODY_MAX), shaped as: 'Langston — weekly plan census <week> at <sha7>. You resolve this row, with evidence naming what you did with each list.' then one line per list (a)-(f), each with its count(s) and up to 3 top items with owners. Owners come from the issue head's '(CC-x' token or the plan row's owner cell. (b) and (c) show the oldest items first; (f) shows NEW only. The final line is 'Full lists: metadata.lists of this alert (scripts/due-alerts.py prints them)'. Example items are dropped first, and counts never, when over the cap. The full lists live in the stored row's metadata, as ids only, key order dedupe_key, source, week, ref, counts, lists, so the 300-character slice Discord shows is the counts. That metadata is bounded well under the 131,072-byte single-argument limit of `bash -lc` (about 1.5 KB at today's sizes), and the add refuses to exceed 64 KB, truncating lists with a stated marker. An optional box copy at /var/lib/governance-checker/census/<week>.json is left to Langston (Q8).
- **Files and lines:** census.mjs (body builder); poller.mjs :408 (metadata)
- **Tests / verification:** Render with a copy of formatAlertTextDiscord (:93-102) plus frameResurface (:168-180) at maximum realistic sizes (every list at 999, top items with long ids): fresh ≤ 1,900 and 2nd re-surface ≤ 1,900. The footer containing the alert id survives the cut. The metadata JSON's first 300 characters contain the counts.
- **falls out of:** CE-A1, CE-A18
- **Ordering / notes (this document):** A box-file copy of the full lists is Question 19.

**P46** · OBJ-4 — **DEPARTS from the part-2 routing ruling** · *source: `s2_4.json` plan item [7]*  
- **What changes:** Mistake pass on the clock. The per-week key is gov-mistakepass:<week>, from the same gate, owner CC-A. The body leads with 'OLD Claude — weekly mistake-pattern pass <week>', pre-assembles the MISTAKE: trailer count by slug since lastMistakePassAt (one `git log <BRANCH> --since=… --grep='^MISTAKE:' --format=%B` call) plus the #754 tripwire grep count, and ends 'run THE WEEKLY PASS in MISTAKE_PATTERNS.md, write the run-log row, resolve with the run-log commit sha'. Routing is RECOMMENDED as category verification / severity info, which is not delivered to Discord, so Langston is not engaged, and it reaches CC-A through the per-turn inject-due-alerts read. If the previous week's row is still live, it escalates to governance/warning, which posts, engages Langston and re-surfaces. This departs from the part-2 ruling ('posts under governance'), because A3 shows that ruling cannot satisfy its own 'not Langston' condition. It is put to Langston (Q1).
- **Files and lines:** poller.mjs (maybeRunWeekly, alertSink.add category param)
- **Tests / verification:** Week N+1 is minted while week N is acknowledged-unresolved (the 8a07c40b failure cannot recur). The unresolved prior week gives governance/warning; otherwise verification/info. The trailer-count helper, given a fixture log, counts slugs and returns 0 on none (the positive control is a fixture with 2 slugs).
- **falls out of:** CE-A3, CE-A4, CE-A20
- **Ordering / notes (this document):** Question 1. Same-severity rule and flag are Question 14. **Corrected at fresh-reader round 1 (§1 FR1-CE-4):** "so Langston is not engaged" above holds for his bridge only. His own per-turn §10.5 read has no category or severity filter, so a `verification`/`info` row is surfaced and triaged in each of his turns until CC-A acks it. The ack is safe here because the key is per-week — acking week N cannot stop week N+1 (CE-A4). Question 1 is restated on that trade-off. *Falls out of (correction):* CE-A4; §1 FR1-CE-4.

## 7. QUESTIONS FOR LANGSTON

Consolidated from all five reads, de-duplicated. **Questions 1-11 DEPART from, or cannot be met as, a Step-1 ruling or an approved scope text — they come first.** Each carries the read's recommendation where it gave one, and the findings it rests on.

### 7.1 Departures from the Step-1 rulings and the approved scope

1. **OBJ-4's routing condition cannot be met by any `governance` alert.** Your part-2 ruling: the pass posts under `governance`, body leads with "OLD Claude", so it wakes CC-A and not you. At the ref: the CC bridge logs only Kyle's messages, so the alert post never reaches the wake filter; the wake filter has no `langston_alert_inbound` branch; your bridge engages every alerts-webhook post whatever its text; and the Discord post begins with a category header, so no body can "lead". CC-A is woken only if your triage names CC-A in prose (144 of 187 = 77% on replayed history — a replay, not observed wakes). Options: (i) keep `governance`, accept that you triage the pass weekly and CC-A wakes only through your prose; (ii) post it as `verification`/`info` — not posted to Discord, so your bridge is not invoked — reaching CC-A through the per-turn alert read, escalating to `governance`/`warning` only when the previous week is still open. ⚠️ *Corrected at fresh-reader round 1 (§1 FR1-CE-4):* you are still **engaged** — your own §10.5 per-turn read has no category or severity filter, so the row is surfaced and triaged in each of your turns, on any topic, until CC-A acks it; (iii) a text-keyed skip in your bridge (Infra's code, a hand-installed file under `#1004`); (iv) if "not Langston" must hold literally, a category or owner exclusion in your own §10.5 filter — outside the checker, and yours or Infra's to change. **Recommendation: (ii), on the corrected trade-off — a surfacing in your per-turn read each week until CC-A claims the row, against a bridge invocation each week under (i). Yours to rule.** *(CE-A1, CE-A2, CE-A3, CE-A4; plan P46.)*
2. **The census cannot say "owner Langston", and leading its body with "Langston" does nothing.** Your alert prompt offers `owner=<CC-A|CC-B|Kyle>` and maps governance to CC-A, and the wake filter's `ALERT_OWNERS` has no Langston — so your weekly census triage will by default assign CC-A and usually name CC-A in prose, waking CC-A every week for your own row. What marker should a census triage carry — e.g. `owner=Kyle action="FYI — Langston resolves the census"`, or an explicit instruction in the census body? Adding `owner=Langston` needs a bridge-prompt and wake-filter change (Infra). *(CE-A1, CE-A2, CE-A5.)*
3. **Q10 put "the heartbeat tests" into CI — none exist.** `heartbeat-check.mjs` has no test file and no injection seam (`checkHeartbeat` reads files and shells out; `poller.test.mjs:406-410` says heartbeat resolves are not exercised), and its single `hb.alertId` and hard-coded `dedupe_key` cannot carry a second alert. The plan (P28) refactors a pure `decideHeartbeat` seam and adds `heartbeat-check.test.mjs` — the first coverage — while keeping the silent-poller path unchanged. The two reads proposed different signatures (P28 carries both). Confirm this refactor is inside this batch. *(HY-A11, CE-A12, CE-A13.)*
4. **The planted-failure CI control cannot be pushed to the shared branch.** It would turn `migration/aws-supabase` red; a PR into it would not trigger CI (HYPOTHESIS: `*` does not match `/`, per GitHub's documented filter rules, not measured). A push to a throwaway `migration/b-plan-currency-check-ci-control` does trigger (`migration/**`). Removing that remote branch afterwards is a delete — it needs Kyle's or your OK before Step 5; the alternative is to leave the branch and name it in the completion report. *(HY-A13; HY open question 5.)*
5. **OBJ-5's approved verification cannot pass as worded.** "A whole-tree grep for either governed path returning nothing" will match the rule-18 `.removed` copies the same objective mandates, and the prose that quotes the paths. Its control (`:143`/`:181`) can never come back from a path grep either: those lines read `io.open(DEST, "w", …)` and contain no path. **Corrected at fresh-reader r1:** this document's first replacement, `git grep -n -E 'DEST *=|io\.open\(DEST' <ref> -- scripts ':!1-system-manual/_archive'`, cannot pass either. It returns **10** lines at `158c66983` and `9d80cd991`, and 6 of them (`lc5-preinstall-exercise.py:42`, `build-codex-repo.mjs:45`, `draft.py:5/:308`, `render.py:5/:67`) survive the deletions in files your ruling leaves. **Proposed replacement:** (1) the four paths are absent from `git ls-tree -r <ref> scripts/inventory/`, which covers the write lines; (2) `git grep -n -F -e 'DawnTraderV3-new\1-system-manual\SPRINT_TO_LIVE_PLAN' -e 'DawnTraderV3-new\Claude Comms and Packages\Scope Files\PRE_LIVE_SPRINT' <ref> -- ':!*.md' ':!1-system-manual/_archive'` returns nothing. It only stops a same-week double mint after a crash before the state save (CE-A7 — the plan also saves state immediately after the add); it never blocks a re-mint after a resolve, so the gate stays authoritative. *(CE-A7, CE-A8, CE-A9; CE open question 4.)*

(HYPOTHESIS for the count: `gov-classundeclared` for F-G-1 and F-G-2; docgap scope and pre_audit for F-G-2 under the architecture default; `gov-ledgerrow` task_lists for F-G-2). Without it, row 16 and the §5 F-G-1 line stay ungradable and fall to the census. **Both results, computed (R4, §1.1):** §4 rows without a parseable id — 116 of 218 without the F-G line, 115 with it (116 / 115 of 214 at `9d80cd991`); census list (d) 5 rows vs 4 (row 16 drops out). 8. **The inclusive bound vs code-less batches (your Q2).** `>=` is required (CD-A2, the one-commit close `07c806722`). But for a batch with no code, `firstLead` is usually its Step-1 commit, so a Step-1 status or naming edit to its own row pays the close obligation (rows 1a, 9a, 9f, 159; this batch's own row was edited at `e5dc4236f` and `b543448c2`, at or after its `firstLead`). Options: (a) accept and record; (b) a `+` line whose id is NEW to the batch cell in that commit (no matching `−` line) does not credit — closes the naming case, not a status edit; (c) for code-less batches, use the latest scope first-add as the bound. (d) *(added after fresh-reader round 1)* a −/+ pair credits only when the row's status or report cell differs between them; a +-only line follows (b). This closes the wider class CD-A14's amendment measured — a note-only or homing edit by ANY commit (26 of 28 −/+ row pairs in the plan's history left status and report unchanged; `17d234293` touched the notes of 9 id-bearing rows, each still QUEUED) — and makes the ordering of this batch's own bulk plan edits (P4, P6, P7, P9, P12) immaterial to crediting. **Recommendation (revised after fresh-reader round 1): (b) and (d) together, plus recording the rest.** Also: the Q2 residual you pre-registered (Step-10 edit then later code) does **not** appear among graded closes in the window — keep the bound, do not widen to `firstCode` (CD-A4). *(CD-A2, CD-A4, CD-A14.)*

### 7.2 Design choices the plan needs ruled

12. ✅ **ANSWERED — R2 (§1.1): one dated-home rule, chosen by what OBJ-8 is for.** A `\bHOME\b` line in an OPEN issue's span with `\bdue\b\W{0,3}(20\d\d-\d\d-\d\d)` (case-insensitive), a match immediately after `"` or `` ` `` excluded as quoted-struck — **30 issues / 39 lines / 41 matches** at both refs. At `9d80cd991`, 39 of the 200 placed-by-number OPEN issues are matched only in a note cell. 27 of those stay placed anyway through a HOME batch id. Excluding note cells from every placement match gives 343/126 instead of 354/115 (347/125 at `d4a2679c4`), which moves 11 issues (10 at `d4a2679c4`). Read one by one, most of the movers are the plan's own note-cell homing form: row 150 '+ `#502` … and `#498`', row 69 '+ `#641`', row 34 '+ `#635`', row 93 'this row IS `#682` (with `#675`); + `#662`', row 137 'carries `#1052`'. Your Q5 standard is 'a prose mention is not placement', and OBJ-1 does not credit an id in a note. Options: (a) count any note-cell mention, as built, and record it; (b) item and batch cells only, which would false-flag those deliberate homings; (c) item and batch cells plus a note cell only in a fixed homing form (`+ #N`, `carries #N`, `this row IS #N`, `with #N`). Recommendation: (a) now, recorded, with (c) as the rule if the plan's note homing form is to be governed. This needs a ruling before the P44 dry run, because the baseline moves with it. (§1 FR1-CE-3; CE-A14; P43, P44.)

**R1-Q14** (OBJ-3 / OBJ-4) — **List (a): the first-fire window.** List (a) is now windowed by ref (files added between the previous census's graded ref and the current ref). The first live census has no previous ref. Options: (a) the flip commit, so nothing before the flip is listed; (b) the branch ref as of 7 days before the first fire, which gives one week of history and has a known positive set (the 4 reports first-added in the 7 days to `9d80cd991`, CE-A17); (c) every completion-named file (~300), which would bury the first live fire. Recommendation: (b). (§1 FR1-CE-9; P43.)

**R1-Q15** (OBJ-3 / OBJ-4) — **The drift check's fail-open shape.** `checkerCodeDrift` wraps every listed file in one `try` and returns `drifted:false` on any rev-parse failure (`poller.mjs:522-533` at `158c66983`; your 2026-07-11 fail-open ruling, comment at :519-520). One listed file that is absent at the ref therefore turns the #490 guard off for all files, and the :604-605 branch resolves an open `gov-code-drift`. The plan fixes this by ORDER (census.mjs joins the list in the commit that creates it) and adds a presence test in CI. Should `hashAt` also treat a path absent at BOTH refs as a no-op, and keep failing open on any other error? That would change the ruled shape, so it is not assumed. (§1 FR1-CE-7; P29, P43.)

*(R1-Q the drift check's fail-open shape was raised by two areas, OBJ-10 and OBJ-3/4; both texts are kept — rule once.)*

## 9. RISKS AND BLAST RADIUS

**Who is reached.** Every session: once OBJ-1 is flipped, any batch whose id is in a §4 batch cell (or a §5 item cell) owes its own line at close; once OBJ-3/OBJ-4 are flipped, Langston and CC-A receive a weekly row. The cost of a wrong rule is alert noise and Langston's triage, not blocked work — the checker stays detect-not-block (scope §7).

**What is live on push, and what is dormant.** The checker clone fast-forwards before every tick (box-only `20-auto-redeploy.conf`), so **a push is a deploy within ~30 minutes, outside `dt-deploy` and the app's hold**, and the box runs `GOV_SHADOW=0` — every alert goes straight to the real queue (CD risk 1, CE risks, HY risk 1). Live on push: the exceptions-parser change and its data edits (P21-P26 — one commit, preview re-run at the push ref), the id-pattern fix (P31 — 0 of the current 300 commits re-attributed, 64 historically), the heartbeat seam and the drift list (P28-P29 — ship together), the CI steps (P30). Dormant until a committed flip: `gov-planline` (P32-P38), the census and the mistake pass (P40-P46). ⚠️ **Live on push means live BEFORE Langston's Step 4**, which runs at the graded ref after the push. For P21-P26, P28-P29 and P31 that departs from the intent of Q9 (review before live), and it is put to Langston (round-1 question; §6.4 note, §1 row 46).

**Named risks, each with where it is handled:**
1. **Ordering of the deletions.** If `order.py` or the working order goes before the after-rewrite (P6) and the Wave 0 carry (P7(a)), 34 references lose their only in-tree key and the one PLS-ahead correction is lost (DL risk 1). This document carries the mapping (DL-A4) and the reconcile table (DL-A6).
2. **A blind census list (e).** Reusing `order.py`'s pattern makes it silent for every converted `after row N` reference (PF-A14); its regex must be proven on the 10 existing ones.
3. **Renames that break references and move exemptions.** Row 16's rename breaks row 100's reference unless one commit (PF-A15); re-pointing rows 35 and 87 moves their ids out of OBJ-1's exemption set (row 55 keeps its exempt id), which P39's third run records at P12's own commit; and P12 must also rewrite both rows' item cells, or P4's check and census list (d) misfire (PF-A16; §1 rows 42-43).
4. **The lower bound.** Pinned `lastCode` would make every pre-report plan edit a miss (CD-A1); the bound must be inclusive (CD-A2); it is window-scoped (CD-A7); and it lets a code-less batch's Step-1 row edit pay its close (CD-A14, Question 8).
5. **The rename in the diff.** Without `--diff-filter=M`, `4f3668af2` credits every batch (CD-A5).
6. **A crash after the census add re-mints the week** unless state is saved at the add (CE-A7); in SHADOW the gate must record the week on a logged add (CE-A23).
7. **The Discord body cap is load-bearing:** an overrun removes the metadata slice (the counts) first, and the alert id only after about 315 more characters (CE-A18, corrected at §1 FR1-CE-6).
8. **Prose-convention parsing.** Every census list rests on conventions in `RUNNING_ISSUES` and the plan; one leading parenthetical moved OPEN by 1; the HOME-id reading moves placement by 18-19 on one grammar, the Question-7 grammar by 2, and dropping note-cell matches by 11 (§1 FR1-CE-1 to FR1-CE-3; the earlier "up to 33" mixed two scripts). The census prints its own parse stats and refuses on zero heads; a rule change goes through Langston. Reach limits: `#532`-shaped heads, `#559` (PF-A1, PF-A17).
9. **Time budget.** The plan diff read adds 0.06-0.18 s (CD-A6); the census adds ~5 git reads including the 3.1 MB ledger, **not yet measured on the box** — the dry run must time it (CE risks).
10. **Wake routing.** Under `governance` the mistake pass engages Langston against his own condition, and the census wakes CC-A through his triage prose (CE-A3, CE-A5); under `verification`/`info` his bridge is not invoked, but his own per-turn §10.5 read still surfaces and triages the row until CC-A acks it (§1 FR1-CE-4) — Questions 1-2.
11. **The plan's layout is load-bearing.** A missing §4 or §5 header line makes the rule fail closed and page (CD risk 4) — intended.
12. **Concurrent edits.** The plan saw 52 commits in about two days and `PRE_LIVE_SPRINT` 14 since 09-29; before/after line figures and recounts are re-derived at each commit's own parent; OBJ-8's CC-A edits sit in a dense interleaved region (RI:424-548) — rule 25.c: read the staged hunk (PF risks 3-4, DL risk 4).
13. **Other owners' rows.** The ledger retypes (L42, L43, L45, L111), the OBJ-9 row edits and the OBJ-8 entries touch other sessions' text — tell them or have them do it (HY risk 2, PF plan items).
14. **The always-loaded Step-10 skill cites `poller.mjs` by line** (:124); this batch's edits move those lines — P52 converts them (:124 and :130) to symbols in the first poller.mjs commit, P21-P26, because that commit already moves `:698` (HY-A16).
15. **Heartbeat.** No tests today; its first coverage is a refactor that must not change the silent-poller path; until the drift list includes it, a failed fast-forward leaves it silently stale (CE-A12, HY risk 3).
16. **Until the deletion lands**, any session that runs `plan_doc.py` or `sort.py` from any folder overwrites CC-B's working copy — measured in §2 (DL risk 5). **The deletion ends only those two.** `draft.py:5`/`:308` and `render.py:5`/`:67` write into CC-B's clone the same way, their inputs exist on this laptop, and the ruling leaves them. So the hazard class survives this batch unless Langston rules otherwise (DL-A15; Question 34).

## 10. PLAIN-LANGUAGE SUMMARY

**What the audit turned up.** The design Langston approved holds, but several of its load-bearing details did not survive contact with the code. The checker's "last code commit" date is quietly replaced by the report date on every closed batch, so the new plan-line check needs two new dates of its own and must count a same-commit close as on time; the plan's own rename would credit every batch unless whole-file additions are skipped. The routing Langston asked for on the mistake-pattern pass — reach Old Claude, not him — cannot be done with a governance alert, because his bridge answers every alert post and the other sessions never see it directly; that needs his call. Several of the scope's counts moved once their rules were written down (open issues, placed issues, dated homes, the "after X" references), and in four places the five reads disagreed because they used different rules. Each is now settled by one written rule, chosen by what that list is for, implemented and run with controls (§1.1): 469 open issues (472 today), 30 issues with a dated home, 35 unmatched "after X" references. The fourth — whether "F-G" batch names parse — is a real choice, so both answers are shown and it is left to Langston. Two claims in the scope do not hold: the `:28` citation never moved, and the confirmer rule lives on line 8, not 9. And one reader's scratch run of the old plan generator overwrote New Claude's working copy of the plan — restored losslessly by Old Claude — which is the clearest possible proof of why those scripts must go.

**What the plan is.** Sixty-three steps in dependency order: first correct the plan by hand (rename and fill in batch ids, rewrite the "after X" notes to row numbers, fix the stale rows and citations) and carry into this document everything the deletions would otherwise destroy; then delete the four old scripts and the duplicate working order; then tighten the exceptions ledger's reader in one commit with its data fixes; then add the checker tests to CI and give the heartbeat something testable; then land the plan-line check and the weekly census switched off; then update the governance documents; and only after Langston's review, a committed preview that must catch `B-XSTOCK-FEE-CONTRACT`, and a dry run, switch each one on and watch it run. Five steps (and one half of a sixth, P51) have no audit behind them yet and are marked UNAUDITED; thirty-three questions go to Langston, the first eleven because the audit could not do what a ruling or the approved scope said (Question 12 and the first half of Question 9 are now answered by written rules).
